In this proof, unlike in the SGHMCLP-F algorithm where gradients are unbiased, additional noise applied to the state
{\mathbf{x}}
causes deviation from the underdamped Langevin dynamics, leading us to establish an intermediate process to address this noise.

Recall the continuous interpolation of the SGHMCLP-L,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\gamma{\mathbf{v}}_{s}ds-u\int_{0}^{t}G_{s}ds+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha_{v}(s)ds

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{v}}_{s}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha_{x}(s)ds,

where
G_{s}=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}Q_{G}\left(\nabla U(x^{\prime}_{k})\right)\bm{1}_{\mathrm{s\in\left(k\eta,\left(k+1\right)\eta\right)}}
.And we define an intermediate process by let
{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}_{t}={\mathbf{v}}_{t}+\alpha_{x}(t)
:

\displaystyle v^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle=v^{\prime}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\gamma\left(v^{\prime}_{s}-\alpha_{x}(s)\right)ds-u\int_{0}^{t}G_{s}ds+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\alpha_{v}(s)+\frac{1}{t}\alpha_{x}(t)\right)ds

\displaystyle x^{\prime}_{t}

\displaystyle=x^{\prime}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}v^{\prime}_{s}ds.

(28)

By integrating the underdamped Langevin dynamic (
10
), we can have:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\gamma\left({\mathbf{v}}_{s}-\alpha_{x}(s)\right)ds-u\int_{0}^{t}\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})ds+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{v}}_{s}ds.

(29)

Notice that the process
x^{\prime}_{t}
has the same distribution with
{\mathbf{x}}_{t}
, thus in the following analysis we study the convergence of the intermediate process
p^{\prime}_{k}=(x^{\prime}_{k\eta},v^{\prime}_{k\eta})
. By taking the difference of equation (
28
) with (
29
) and the Girsanov formula, we can derive the Radon-Nikodym derivative of
\hat{P}_{K\eta}
w.r.t
p^{\prime}_{K}
:

\displaystyle\frac{d\hat{p}_{K\eta}}{dp^{\prime}_{K}}=exp\left\{\sqrt{\frac{u}{2\gamma}}\int_{0}^{T}(\gamma\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)+\frac{1}{T}\alpha_{x}(T)+\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s})d\mathbf{B}s\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{u}{4\gamma}\int_{0}^{T}\|\gamma\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)+\frac{1}{T}\alpha_{x}(T)+\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}\|^{2}ds\right\}.

Thus the divergence can be bouned as:

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})=\mathbb{E}_{p_{K}}\left[\log\left(\frac{d\hat{p}_{K\eta}}{dp_{K}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4\gamma}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left\|\gamma\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)+\frac{1}{T}\alpha_{x}(T)+\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}\right\|^{2}ds

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4\gamma T}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{x}(T)\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma\alpha_{v}(s)+\alpha_{x}(s)+\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma T\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma\alpha_{v}(s)\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{x}(s)\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma T\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-Q_{G}(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma T\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

(30)

\displaystyle+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-Q_{G}(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]ds.

By assumption
1
, we know that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq M^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right].

From the same analysis in (
25
), we can derive:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq 3M^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+u^{2}\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]+2du\right).

Now we need to derive a uniform bound of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
.

Lemma 11
.

Let Assumptions
2
and
1
hold. If we set the step size to the following condition

\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{\gamma}{4\left(8Mu+u\gamma+22\gamma^{2}\right)},\sqrt{\frac{4u^{2}}{4Mu+3\gamma^{2}}},\frac{6\gamma bu}{\left(4Mu+3\gamma^{2}\right)d},\frac{\gamma m_{2}}{6\left(22u+\gamma\right)M^{2}}\right\},

then for all
k>0

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
can be bouned as follow:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathcal{E}+C\Delta^{2}d,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v^{\prime}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\gamma^{2}\mathcal{E}/2+\gamma^{2}C\Delta^{2}d/2,

where constants
\mathcal{E}
and
C
are defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}({\mathbf{x}}_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0})\right]+\frac{54\left(4u+\gamma^{2}\right)u}{m_{2}\gamma^{4}}\sigma^{2}+\frac{12(22u+\gamma)uM^{3}}{m_{2}\gamma^{3}}G^{2}+\frac{96\left(d+b\right)uM}{m_{2}\gamma^{2}}

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\frac{27\left(4u+\gamma^{2}\right)u}{2m_{2}\gamma^{4}}.

The proof of Lemma
11
can be found in Appendix
F.5
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3M^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}+2M^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+2G^{2}\right)+2du\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3M^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\gamma^{2}\mathcal{E}/2+\gamma^{2}C\Delta^{2}d/2+u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}+2M^{2}\mathcal{E}+2M^{2}C\Delta^{2}d+2G^{2}\right)+2du\right)

\displaystyle\leq 3M^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)C\Delta^{2}d+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right).

Now we can go back to the divergence of
p_{K}
and
\hat{p}_{K\eta}
,

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma T\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle+\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)C\Delta^{2}d+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)C\Delta^{2}d+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{u\Delta^{2}d}{16\gamma T\eta^{2}}+\frac{uK\Delta^{2}d}{8\gamma\eta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}C\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)+\frac{uK\eta}{16\gamma}+\frac{u}{16\gamma T\eta^{2}}+\frac{uK}{8\gamma\eta}\right)\Delta^{2}d

\displaystyle=:C_{0}K\eta^{3}+C_{1}K\eta\sigma^{2}+C_{2}K\Delta^{2},

where the constants
C_{0}
,
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are defined as:

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4\gamma}

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}\eta^{3}C\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)+\frac{u}{16\gamma}+\frac{u}{16\gamma T^{2}\eta}+\frac{u}{8\gamma\eta}\right)d.

By the weighted CKP inequality and given
K\eta\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})}+\sqrt[4]{D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}\sqrt{\eta}+\widetilde{C_{1}}\widetilde{A}\right)\sqrt{K\eta}+\widetilde{C_{2}}\sqrt{K\Delta},

(31)

where the constants are defined as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{0}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{C_{0}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{C_{1}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\sqrt{C_{2}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\sigma,\sqrt{\sigma}\right\}.

From the same analysis in (
26
), we can have:

\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})\leq\overline{\Lambda}\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}\sqrt{\eta}+\widetilde{C_{1}}\widetilde{A}\right)\sqrt{K\eta}+\widetilde{C_{2}}\sqrt{K\eta}+\Gamma_{0}e^{-\mu^{*}K\eta}.

(32)

In order to bound the Wasserstein distance, we need to set

\overline{\Lambda}\widetilde{C_{0}}\sqrt{K\eta^{2}}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Gamma_{0}e^{-\mu^{*}K\eta}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(33)

Solving the equation (
33
), we can have

K\eta=\frac{\log\left(\frac{2\Gamma_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)}{\mu^{*}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}K\eta}.

Combining these two we can have

\eta=\frac{\epsilon^{2}\mu^{*}}{4\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}\log\left(\frac{2\Gamma_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K=\frac{4\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{2\Gamma_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}\left(\mu^{*}\right)^{2}}.

Plugging in (
32
) completes the proof.

E.3
Proof of Theorem
3

In this section, we analyze the convergence of VC SGHMCLP-L when the target distribution is non-log-concave.
Similarly, the gradients are unbiased, additional noise applied to the state
{\mathbf{x}}
causes deviation from the underdamped Langevin dynamics.
This proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
2
, however, the variance-corrected quantization function gives us a bound for the difference between the quantized value and the full-precision value. This bound can scale with the learning rate. This fact leads to the advantage of variance-corrected quantization over naive stochastic rounding.

Similarily, from the analysis in (
57
), we know that

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\gamma\eta\mathcal{A},

(34)

where
A=\max\left\{\Delta\sqrt{d}\left(A^{\prime}+\mathcal{G}\right),4ud\right\}
.
By the analysis in (
55
), we know that if
\mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{hmc}\geq\frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}
, we can have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq 4ud\eta^{2}

(35)

by (
58
), if
\mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{hmc}<\frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\eta B,

(36)

where
B=\max\left\{2\Delta\sqrt{d}A^{\prime}+u\eta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G},4ud\eta\right\}
.
Thus, we can define the following:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]=\eta\mathcal{B},

(37)

where
\mathcal{B}
is defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=\begin{cases}4ud\eta,&\text{if }\mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{hmc}\geq\frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}\\
B,&\text{else}.\end{cases}

Combining the bound of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]
with (
30
), we can show,

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma T\eta^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{u}{4\gamma}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle+\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)C\Delta^{2}d+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)C\Delta^{2}d+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{u\mathcal{B}}{4\gamma T}+\frac{uK\mathcal{A}}{4}+\frac{uK\mathcal{B}}{4\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)C\Delta^{2}d+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{uK\mathcal{A}}{4}+\frac{uK\mathcal{B}}{2\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}K\eta^{3}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)+\frac{u}{4\gamma}K\eta\sigma^{2}+\frac{u}{16\gamma}K\eta\Delta^{2}d+\frac{uK\mathcal{A}}{4}+\frac{uK\mathcal{B}}{2\gamma}

\displaystyle=:C_{0}K\eta^{3}+C_{1}K\eta\sigma^{2}+C_{2}K\eta\Delta^{2}+C_{3}K\mathcal{A}+C_{4}K\mathcal{B},

where the constants are defined as

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4\gamma}3M^{2}\left(\left(\gamma^{2}+2u^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathcal{E}+u^{2}\sigma^{2}+2u^{2}G^{2}+2du\right)

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4\gamma}

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{16\gamma}d

\displaystyle C_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{4}

\displaystyle C_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{2\gamma}.

By the weighted CKP inequality and given
K\eta\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})}+\sqrt[4]{D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}\sqrt{\eta}+\widetilde{C_{1}}\widetilde{A}+\widetilde{C_{2}}\sqrt{\Delta}\right)\sqrt{K\eta}+\widetilde{C_{3}}\sqrt{K\mathcal{A}}+\widetilde{C_{4}}\sqrt{K\mathcal{B}},

where the constants are defined as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{0}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{C_{0}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{1}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{C_{1}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{2}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{C_{2}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{3}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{C_{3}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{4}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\Lambda}\left(\sqrt{C_{4}}+\sqrt[4]{C_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}^{2}

\displaystyle=\overline{\Lambda}\max\left\{\sigma^{2},\sqrt{\sigma^{2}}\right\}.

From the same analysis of (
26
), we can have:

\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})\leq\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}\sqrt{\eta}+\widetilde{C_{1}}\widetilde{A}\right)\sqrt{K\eta}+\widetilde{C_{2}}\sqrt{K\eta}\Delta+\widetilde{C_{3}}\sqrt{K\mathcal{A}}+\widetilde{C_{4}}\sqrt{K\mathcal{B}}+\Gamma_{0}e^{-\mu^{*}K\eta}.

(38)

To bound the Wasserstein distance, we need to set

\overline{\Lambda}\widetilde{C_{0}}\sqrt{K\eta^{2}}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\Gamma_{0}e^{-\mu^{*}K\eta}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(39)

Solving the equation (
39
), we can have

K\eta=\frac{\log\left(\frac{2\Gamma_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)}{\mu^{*}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta=\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}K\eta}.

Combining these two we can have

\eta=\frac{\epsilon^{2}\mu^{*}}{4\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}\log\left(\frac{2\Gamma_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K=\frac{4\overline{\Lambda}^{2}\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}\log^{2}\left(\frac{2\Gamma_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}\left(\mu^{*}\right)^{2}}.

Plugging in (
38
) completes the proof.

E.4
Proof of Theorem
4

In this section, we analyze the convergence of full-precision gradient accumulators (SGHMCLP-F) introduced in Section
3.1
when the target distribution is strongly log-concave. Again SGHMCLP-F uses biased gradients because we quantize the parameter before taking the gradients. We need to derive the upper bound given biased gradients.

The SGHMCLP-F update follows

\displaystyle{\textnormal{v}}{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}(Q_{W}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle{\textnormal{v}}{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}){\mathbf{v}}_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}(Q_{W}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}},

In this section, we prove the convergence of SGHMCLP-F in terms of
2
-Wasserstein distance for strongly-log-concave target distribution via coupling argument. To simplify the notation we define the quantized stochastic gradients at
{\mathbf{x}}
as:

\displaystyle\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}})

\displaystyle:=Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}(Q_{W}({\mathbf{x}})))

\displaystyle=:\nabla U({\mathbf{x}})+\xi.

(40)

Lemma 12
.

For any
{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, the random noise
\xi
of the low-precision gradients defined in (
40
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\xi\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq M^{2}\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\left\|\xi\right\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq(M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}.

The proof of Lemma
12
can be found in Appendix
F.1
.
We follow the proof in
Cheng et al. 2018
. Denote by
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
the Borel
\sigma
-field of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Given probability measures
\mu
and
\nu
on
(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, we define a
transference plan

\zeta
between
\mu
and
\nu
as a probability measure on
(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}))
such that for all sets
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\zeta(A\times\mathbb{R}^{d})=\mu(A)
and
\zeta(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times A)=\nu(A)
. We denote
\Gamma(\mu,\nu)
as the set of all transference plans. A pair of random variables
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})
is called a coupling if there exists a
\zeta\in\Gamma(\mu,\nu)
such that
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})
is distributed according to
\zeta
. (With some abuse of notation, we will also refer to
\zeta
as the coupling.)

To calculate the Wasserstein distance from the proposed sample
({\mathbf{x}}_{K},{\mathbf{v}}_{K})
and the target distribution sample
({\mathbf{x}}^{*},{\mathbf{v}}^{*})
, we define sample
q_{k}=({\mathbf{x}}_{k},{\mathbf{x}}_{k}+{\mathbf{v}}_{k})
and the target distribution sample
q^{*}=({\mathbf{x}}^{*},{\mathbf{x}}^{*}+{\mathbf{v}}^{*})
. Let
p_{k}=({\mathbf{x}}_{k},{\mathbf{v}}_{k})
and
\widehat{\Phi}_{\eta}
be the operator that maps from
p_{k}
to
p_{k+1}
i.e.

p_{k+1}=\widehat{\Phi}_{\eta}p_{k}.

The solution
({\mathbf{x}}_{t},{\mathbf{v}}_{t})
of the continuous underdamped Langevin dynamics with exact gradient satisfies the following equations:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{0}e^{-\gamma t}-u\left(\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})ds\right)+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s},

(41)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{s}ds.

Let
\Phi_{\eta}
denote the operator that maps
p_{0}
to the solution of continuous underdamped Langevin dynamics in (
41
) after time step
\eta
. Notice the solution
(\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{t},\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t})
of the discrete underdamped Langevin dynamics as in (
10
) with an exact gradient can be written as

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{0}e^{-\gamma t}-u\left(\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}\nabla U(\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{0})ds\right)+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s},

(42)

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{s}ds.

We can also define a similar operator for the discrete underdamped Langevin dynamics solution
\tilde{p}_{t}=(\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t},\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{t})
, let
\widetilde{\Phi}_{t}
be the operator that maps
\tilde{p}_{0}
to
\tilde{p}_{t}
.
Furthermore the SGHMCLP-F can be written as:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{0}e^{-\gamma t}-u\left(\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{0})ds\right)+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s},

(43)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{v}}_{s}ds.

Given
\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{0})=\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{0})+\xi_{0}
and
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}=\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{0}
, we know:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathbf{v}}}_{t}-u\left(\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}ds\right)\xi

(44)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathbf{x}}}_{t}-u\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}ds\right)dr\right)\xi.

Lemma 13
.

Let
q_{0}
be some initial distribution and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}
and
\Phi_{\eta}
be the operator we defined above for discrete Langevin dynamics with exact full-precision gradients and low-precision gradients respectively. If the stepszie
1>\eta>0
, then the Wasserstein distance satisfies

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*})\leq\left(\mathcal{W}_{2}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*})+\sqrt{5}/2u\eta\sqrt{d}M\Delta\right)^{2}+5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right).

The proof of Lemma
13
can be found in Appendix
F.2
.
The lemma
13
says that if starting from the same distribution after one step of low-precision update the Wasserstein distance from the target distribution is bounded by the distance after one step of exact gradients plus
\mathcal{O}(\eta^{2}\Delta^{2})
. Furthermore from the corollary 7 in
Cheng et al. 2018
we know that for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,K\}
:

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})\leq e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(q_{i},q^{*}),

(45)

where
\kappa_{1}=M/m_{1}
is the condtion number.
Let
\mathcal{E}_{K}
denote the
26\left(d/m_{1}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)
, and from the discretization error bound from Theorem 9 and Lemma 8 (sandwich inequality) in
Cheng et al. 2018
, we get

\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i})\leq 2\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}p_{i},\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}p_{i})\leq\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}.

By triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i})+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{i},q^{*}).

Combine this with the result in Lemma
13
we have,

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})\leq\left(e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{i},q^{*})+\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{5}/2u\eta\sqrt{d}M\Delta\right)^{2}+5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right).

(46)

By invoking the Lemma 7 in
Dalalyan & Karagulyan 2019
we can bound the
2
-Wasserstein distance by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{K},q^{*})

\displaystyle\leq e^{-K\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})+\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}.

Finally, by sandwich inequality we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-K\eta/2\kappa}\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{0},p^{*})+4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa}}

\displaystyle+\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/\kappa}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}.

Now we let the first term less than
\epsilon/3
, from the lemma 13 in
(
Cheng et al. 2018
)
we know that
\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})\leq 3\left(\frac{d}{m_{1}}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)
. So we can choose
K
as the following,

K\leq\frac{2\kappa_{1}}{\eta}\log\left(36\left(\frac{d}{m_{1}}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)\right).

Next, we choose a step size
\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon\kappa_{1}^{-1}}{\sqrt{479232/5(d/m_{1}+\mathcal{D}^{2})}}
to ensure the second term is controlled below
\epsilon/3+\frac{16\kappa_{1}uM\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}
. Since
1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\geq\eta/4\kappa_{1}
and definition of
\mathcal{E}_{K}
,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}}{\eta/4\kappa_{1}}\leq 16\kappa_{1}\left(\eta\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{uM\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon/3+\frac{16\kappa_{1}uM\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}.

Finally by choosing the step size satisfied that,

\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{120u\left[(M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right]},

the third term can be bounded as:

\displaystyle\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/\kappa}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}{\frac{u\eta M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}{2}}=40u\eta\frac{\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}{M\Delta\sqrt{5d}}\leq\epsilon/3.

This complete the proof.

E.5
Proof of Theorem
5

In this section, we analyze the convergence of SGHMCLP-L when the target distribution is strongly log-concave. We mainly follow the proof in
Cheng et al. 2018
, the difference is we need to handle the noise.

Recall the SGHMCLP-L update rule:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=Q_{W}\left({\textnormal{v}}{\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=Q_{W}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}){\mathbf{v}}_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right).

If we let
\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}
and
\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}
denote the quantization error,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}

\displaystyle=Q_{W}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{s}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right)-\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{s}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle=Q_{W}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{s}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta})v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{s}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right)

\displaystyle-\left({\mathbf{x}}_{s}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta})v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{s}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right),

we can rewrite the update rule as:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{s}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}+\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}){\mathbf{v}}_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}+\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}.

(47)

Similarly, we can define a continuous interpolation of (
47
) for
t\in(0,\eta]
.

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{0}e^{-\gamma t}-u\left(\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}\left(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{0})+\zeta\right)ds\right)+\sqrt{2\gamma u}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\gamma(t-s)}dB_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha_{v}(s)ds

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{t}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbf{v}}_{s}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha_{x}(s)ds,

(48)

where the
\zeta=Q_{G}\left(\widetilde{\nabla U}(\hat{x}_{0})\right)-\widetilde{\nabla U}(\hat{x}_{0})
the function
\alpha_{v}(s)
,
\alpha_{x}(s)
are defined as:

\displaystyle\alpha_{v}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}/\eta\bm{1}_{\mathrm{s\in\left(k\eta,(k+1)\eta\right)}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{x}(s)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}/\eta\bm{1}_{\mathrm{s\in\left(k\eta,\left(k+1\right)\eta\right)}}.

If we let
\hat{p}_{0}=(\hat{x}_{0},\hat{v}_{0})
be the initial sample and
\hat{p}_{t}=\left(\hat{x}_{t},\hat{v}_{t}\right)
be the sample that satisfies the previous equations, we can define an operator
\hat{\Phi}_{t}
that maps
\hat{p}_{0}
to
\hat{p}_{t}
i.e.,
\hat{p}_{t}=\hat{\Phi}_{t}\hat{p}_{0}
. Notice that since
\hat{p}_{t}
is the continuous interpolation of (
6
), thus
\hat{p}_{k\eta}=p_{k}=\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k},v_{k}\right)
. Similarly, we define
q_{k}=({\mathbf{x}}_{k},v_{k}+{\mathbf{x}}_{k})=:({\mathbf{x}}_{k},\omega_{k})
as a tool to analyze the convergence of
p_{k}
.

We are now ready to compute the Wasserstein distance between
\hat{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0}
and
q^{*}
. Let
\Gamma_{1}
be all of the couplings between
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0}
and
q^{*}
, and
\Gamma_{2}
be all of the couplings between
\widehat{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0}
and
q^{*}
. Let
r_{1}
be the optimal coupling between
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0}
and
q^{*}
. By taking the difference between (
48
) and (
42
),

\begin{bmatrix}x\\
\omega\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{x}\\
\widetilde{\omega}\end{bmatrix}+u\begin{bmatrix}\left(\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr\right)\zeta+\int_{0}^{\eta}\alpha_{x}(s)ds\\
\left(\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr+\int_{0}^{\eta}e^{-\gamma(s-\eta)}ds\right)\zeta+\int_{0}^{\eta}\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)ds\end{bmatrix}.

Let us now analyze the Wasserstein distance between
\hat{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0}
and
q^{*}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\hat{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*}\right)

(49)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{r_{1}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{x}\\
\widetilde{\omega}\end{bmatrix}+u\begin{bmatrix}\left(\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr\right)\zeta+\int_{0}^{\eta}\alpha_{x}(s)ds\\
\left(\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr+\int_{0}^{\eta}e^{-\gamma(s-\eta)}ds\right)\zeta+\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)\right)ds\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}x^{*}\\
\omega^{*}\end{bmatrix}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{r_{1}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{x}\\
\widetilde{\omega}\end{bmatrix}-\begin{bmatrix}x^{*}\\
\omega^{*}\end{bmatrix}\right\|^{2}+u^{2}\mathbb{E}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\left(\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr\right)\zeta+\int_{0}^{\eta}\alpha_{x}(s)ds\\
\left(\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr+\int_{0}^{\eta}e^{-\gamma(s-\eta)}ds\right)\zeta+\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)\right)ds\end{bmatrix}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*}\right)+4u^{2}\left(\left(\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\gamma(s-r)}ds\right)dr\right)^{2}+\left(\int_{0}^{\delta}e^{-\gamma(s-\delta)}ds\right)^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+u^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\alpha_{x}(s)\right)ds\right\|^{2}\right]+u^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\int_{0}^{\eta}\left(\alpha_{x}(s)+\alpha_{v}(s)\right)ds\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*}\right)+4u^{2}\left(\frac{\eta^{4}}{4}+\eta^{2}\right)\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+u^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]+u^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}+\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*}\right)+5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{0},q^{*}\right)+5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\left(A+B\right),

(50)

where the constant
A
,
B
are the uniform bounds of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|\right]
respectively. Furthermore from the corollary 7 in
Cheng et al. 2018
we know that for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,K\}
:

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})\leq e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(q_{i},q^{*}),

(51)

where
\kappa_{1}=M/m_{1}
is the condtion number.
From the discretization error bound from theorem 9 and lemma 8(sandwich inequality) in
Cheng et al. 2018
, we get

\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i})\leq 2\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}p_{i},\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}p_{i})\leq\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}.

By triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},\widetilde{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i})+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\Phi_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{i},q^{*}),

further implies the following inequality:

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\left(\hat{\Phi}_{\eta}q_{i},q^{*}\right)\leq\left(e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}\left(q_{i},q^{*}\right)+\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}\right)^{2}+5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\left(A+B\right).

(52)

By invoking the Lemma 7 in
Dalalyan & Karagulyan 2019
we can bound the Wasserstein distance by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{K},q^{*})

\displaystyle\leq e^{-K\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})+\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\left(A+B\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\left(A+B\right)}}.

Finally, by sandwich inequality we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-K\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})+\frac{4\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}

(53)

\displaystyle+\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+8u^{2}\left(A+B\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\left(A+B\right)}}.

And in this case, we know that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|\right]
can be bouned by
\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}
. Finally, we can have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-K\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})+\frac{4\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+4u^{2}\Delta^{2}d}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+u^{2}\Delta^{2}d}}.

Now we let the first term less than
\epsilon/3
, from the lemma 13 in
(
Cheng et al. 2018
)
we know that
\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})\leq 3\left(\frac{d}{m_{1}}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)
. So we can choose
K
as the following,

K\leq\frac{2\kappa_{1}}{\eta}\log\left(36\left(\frac{d}{m_{1}}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)\right).

Next, we choose a step size
\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon\kappa_{1}^{-1}}{\sqrt{479232/5(d/m_{1}+\mathcal{D}^{2})}}
to ensure the second term is controlled below
\epsilon/3
. Since
1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\geq\eta/4\kappa_{1}
and definition of
\mathcal{E}_{K}
,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{\eta/4\kappa_{1}}\leq 16\kappa_{1}\left(\eta\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}\right)\leq\epsilon/3.

Finally by choosing the step size satisfied that,

\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2880\kappa_{1}u\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)},

the third term can be bounded as:

\displaystyle\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+4u^{2}\Delta^{2}d}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+4u^{2}\Delta^{2}d}{\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}\leq\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+4u^{2}\Delta^{2}d}{\sqrt{\eta/4\kappa_{1}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{20\kappa_{1}u^{2}\eta\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}+\frac{8u^{2}\Delta^{2}d\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}}{\eta^{3/2}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon/3+\frac{8u^{2}\Delta^{2}d\sqrt{\kappa_{1}}}{\eta^{3/2}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)}}.

This completes the proof.

E.6
Proof of Theorem
6

In this section, we analyze the convergence of VC SGHMCLP-L when the target distribution is strongly log-concave.
This proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
5
, however, the variance-corrected quantization function gives us a bound for the difference between the quantized value and the full-precision value.
This bound can scale with the learning rate. This fact leads to the advantage of variance-corrected quantization over naive stochastic rounding.

Recall the VC SGHMCLP-L update rule is the following,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=Q^{vc}\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}\left(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right),Var_{v},\Delta\right)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=Q^{vc}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}\left(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})),Var_{x},\Delta\right).

(54)

If we let
\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}
and
\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}
denote the quantization error,

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}=

\displaystyle Q^{vc}\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}\left(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right),Var_{v},\Delta\right)-\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right)

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}=

\displaystyle Q^{vc}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}\left(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})),Var_{x},\Delta\right)

\displaystyle-\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta})v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right),

we can rewrite the update rule as:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}+\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\eta})v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}+\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}.

Next, we first derive a uniform bound of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]
.
In this section and the following section, we further assume the norm of quantized stochastic gradients are bounded.

Assumption 5
.

For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, there exists a constant
\mathcal{G}
and the quantized stochastic gradients at
x
satisfies the following

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}(x))\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathcal{G}^{2}.

By the definition of the variance corrected quantization function
Q^{vc}
, when
Var_{v}>\rho_{0}=\frac{\Delta^{2}}{4}
, if we let
\psi_{k}
denote
v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}\left(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\middle|\psi_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)+\sqrt{Var_{v}}\xi_{k}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-Q^{d}\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\sqrt{Var_{v}-\rho_{0}}\xi_{k}\right)-\text{sign}(r)c\right\|^{2}\middle|\psi_{k}\right]

Let

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=Q^{d}\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\sqrt{Var_{v}-\rho_{0}}\xi_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt-\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\sqrt{Var_{v}-\rho_{0}}\xi_{k}\right),

then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\middle|\psi_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)+\sqrt{Var_{v}}\xi_{k}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\left(v_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\sqrt{Var_{v}-\rho_{0}}\xi_{k}\right)-b-\text{sign}(r)c\right\|^{2}\middle|\psi_{k}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sqrt{Var_{v}}\xi_{k}-\sqrt{Var_{v}-\rho_{0}}\xi_{k}-b-\text{sign}(r)c\right\|^{2}\middle|\psi_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sqrt{Var_{v}}\xi_{k}-\sqrt{Var_{v}-\rho_{0}}\xi_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|b+\text{sign}(r)c\right\|^{2}\middle|\psi_{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2Var_{v}d-\rho_{0}d+\rho_{0}d

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\gamma ud\eta.

(55)

When
Var_{v}<\frac{\Delta_{W}^{2}}{4}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)-{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}+\sqrt{Var_{v}}\xi_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)-{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sqrt{Var_{v}}\xi_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max\left(2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)-Q^{s}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)\right\|^{2}\right],2Var_{v}d\right).

(56)

Using the bound equation (6) in
Li & De Sa 2019
gives us,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)-Q^{s}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}-u\gamma^{-1}Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)\sqrt{d}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|\right]\right).

Now we need to derive a uniform bound of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|\right]
,
by the update rule, we know that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}(1-e^{\gamma\eta})Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\xi_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}+\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\gamma\eta/2\right)(1-\gamma\eta/2)^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+\left(\frac{2}{\gamma\eta}+1\right)u^{2}\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U})\right\|^{2}\right]+2\gamma ud\eta+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\gamma\eta/2\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+2\gamma ud\eta+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right].

When
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq 2Var_{v}d<4\gamma ud\eta
, the inequality can be further written as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\gamma\eta/2\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+6\gamma ud\eta

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{6u^{2}\eta\mathcal{G}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\eta}+\frac{12\gamma ud\eta}{\gamma\eta}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{6u^{2}\eta\mathcal{G}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+12ud.

If
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)-Q^{s}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)\right\|^{2}\right]
, the ineuqality can be wirtten as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\gamma\eta/2\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+2\gamma ud\eta+2\Delta\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)\sqrt{d}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\gamma\eta/2\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+2\gamma ud\eta+2\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}\left(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\mathcal{G}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sqrt{1-\gamma\eta/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\frac{\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma\eta/2}}\right)^{2}+3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+2\gamma ud\eta+2\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|\right]\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\frac{\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}}{\left(1-\sqrt{1-\gamma\eta/2}\right)\sqrt{1-\gamma\eta/2}}+\frac{3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+2\gamma ud\eta+2\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}}{\frac{\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma\eta/2}}+\sqrt{\gamma\eta/2\left(3u^{2}\eta/\gamma\mathcal{G}^{2}+2\gamma ud\eta+2\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\frac{\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}}{1-\gamma\eta/2}+\sqrt{6u^{2}/\gamma^{2}\mathcal{G}^{2}+4ud+4\Delta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\Delta\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{6u^{2}/\gamma^{2}\mathcal{G}^{2}+4ud+4\Delta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}}.

Finally, we can have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|\right]\leq

\displaystyle\max\left\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\Delta\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{6u^{2}/\gamma^{2}\mathcal{G}^{2}+4ud+4\Delta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}},\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]}+\sqrt{\frac{6u^{2}\eta\mathcal{G}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}+\sqrt{12ud}\right\}=:A^{\prime}.

Thus, we can have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)-Q^{s}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{k}e^{-\gamma\eta}-u\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right)\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}\left(A^{\prime}+\mathcal{G}\right),

and we can bound the
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]
as,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{v}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{\Delta\gamma\eta\sqrt{d}\left(A^{\prime}+\mathcal{G}\right),4\gamma ud\eta\right\}

\displaystyle=\gamma\eta\max\left\{\Delta\sqrt{d}\left(A^{\prime}+\mathcal{G}\right),4ud\right\}

\displaystyle=:\gamma\eta A.

(57)

Now we bound the
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]
.
When
Var_{x}\geq\rho_{0}
, as the same analysis in (
55
) we can show,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq 2Var_{x}d\leq 4ud\eta^{2}.

If
Var_{x}<\rho_{0}
, and let
\mu_{x}={\mathbf{x}}_{k}+\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}\left(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))
, by the same analysis in (
56
) we can have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mu_{x}-Q^{s}\left(\mu_{x}\right)\right\|^{2}\right],2Var_{x}d\right\}.

Again using the bound equation (6) in
Li & De Sa 2019
gives us,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mu_{x}-Q^{s}(\mu_{x})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\gamma^{-1}\left(1-e^{-\gamma\eta}\right)v_{k}+u\gamma^{-2}\left(\gamma\eta+e^{-\gamma\eta}-1\right)Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\eta\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|_{1}\right]+\frac{u\eta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\eta\sqrt{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|v_{k}\right\|\right]+\frac{u\eta^{2}}{2}\sqrt{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Delta\eta\sqrt{d}A^{\prime}+\frac{u\eta^{2}}{2}\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G}.

Thus, we can have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}^{{\mathbf{x}}}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{2\Delta\eta\sqrt{d}A^{\prime}+u\eta^{2}\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G},4ud\eta^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\max\left\{2\Delta\sqrt{d}A^{\prime}+u\eta\sqrt{d}\mathcal{G},4ud\eta\right\}

\displaystyle=:\eta B.

(58)

Then follow the same analysis of (
53
), we can show

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{-K\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})+\frac{4\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}

(59)

\displaystyle+\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+8u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}}.

(60)

Now we let the first term less than
\epsilon/3
, from the Lemma 13 in
(
Cheng et al. 2018
)
we know that
\mathcal{W}_{2}(q_{0},q^{*})\leq 3\left(\frac{d}{m_{1}}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)
. So we can choose
K
as the following,

K\leq\frac{2\kappa_{1}}{\eta}\log\left(36\left(\frac{d}{m_{1}}+\mathcal{D}^{2}\right)\right).

Next, we choose a step size
\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon\kappa_{1}^{-1}}{\sqrt{479232/5(d/m_{1}+\mathcal{D}^{2})}}
to ensure the second term is controlled below
\epsilon/3
. Since
1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}\geq\eta/4\kappa_{1}
and definition of
\mathcal{E}_{K}
,

\displaystyle 4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{1-e^{-\eta/2\kappa_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}}{\eta/4\kappa_{1}}\leq 16\kappa_{1}\left(\eta\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}\right)\leq\epsilon/3.

Finally choosing the step size satisfied that,

\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{2880\kappa_{1}u\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)},

the third term can be bounded as:

\displaystyle\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+8u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}{\eta^{2}\sqrt{\frac{8\mathcal{E}_{K}}{5}}+\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+8u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}{\sqrt{1-e^{-\eta/\kappa_{1}}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}}\leq\frac{20u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+8u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}{\sqrt{\eta/4\kappa_{1}}\sqrt{5u^{2}\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+2u^{2}\eta\left(\gamma A+B\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{20u^{2}\kappa_{1}\eta\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+8\kappa_{1}u^{2}\left(\gamma A+B\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon/3+8\sqrt{2\kappa_{1}u^{2}\left(\gamma A+B\right)}.

This completes the proof.

E.7
Proof of Thoerem
7

In this section we generalize the convergence analysis of LPSGLDLP-F in
Zhang et al. 2022
to non-log-concave target distribution. We prove a more general version of theorem
7
following the same proof outlines in
Raginsky et al. 2017
. We further introduce an assumption about the initial distribution
p_{0}
.

Assumption 6
.

The probability
p_{0}
of the initial hypothesis
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}
has a bounded and strictly positive density and satisfies the following:

\kappa_{0}:=\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{\left\|x\right\|^{2}}p_{0}(x)dx<\infty.

Note that the for initial distribution
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}=0
, the value
\kappa_{0}=0
is bounded and the assumption is satisfied. Recall the Overdamped Langevin dynamics is

d{\mathbf{x}}_{t}=-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{t})dt+\sqrt{2}dB_{t}.

(61)

We further define the value of the energy function and the gradient at point
0
at the following:

|U(0)|=G_{0},\quad\left\|\nabla U(0)\right\|=G_{1}.

In order to analyze the convergence of SGLD for non-log-concave distribution, we need to introduce extra assumptions.

Then the solution of the Langevin dynamics should satisfies

{\mathbf{x}}_{t}={\mathbf{x}}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})ds+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}dB_{s}.

(62)

To analyze the LPSGLDLP-F in (
1
), we define a continuous interpolation of the low-precision sample as:

\hat{x}_{t}=\hat{x}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}G_{s}ds+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}dB_{s}.

(63)

where
G_{s}=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{K}\tilde{g}(\hat{x}_{k})\bm{1}_{\mathrm{s\in[k\eta,(k+1)\eta)}}
. The Wasserstein distance can be bounded as

\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})\leq\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\hat{p}_{K\eta},p^{*}).

(64)

Now, we are ready to introduce the contraction rate
\lambda^{*}
of the overdamped Langevin dynamics (
13
). By borrowing the proposition 9 of
(
Raginsky et al. 2017
)
, we can have:

Lemma 14

(Proposittion 9 in
Raginsky et al. 2017
)
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
and
2
hold. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(\hat{p}_{K\eta},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2C_{LS}\left(\log\left\|p_{0}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{d}{2}\log\frac{3\pi}{m}+\left(\frac{M\kappa_{0}}{3}+B\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}+G_{0}+\frac{b}{2}\log 3\right)\right)}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}},

(65)

with constant

C_{LS}\leq\frac{2m_{2}^{2}+8M^{2}}{m_{2}^{2}M}+\frac{1}{\lambda^{*}}\left(\frac{6Md}{m_{2}}+2\right).

where the
\lambda^{*}=e^{-\tilde{\mathcal{O}}(d)}
denotes the contraction rate of overdamped Langevin dynamics.

Here,
\lambda^{*}
acts as a contraction rate of the Markov process initiated by (
13
), with an exponential dependency on the dimension
d
being inescapable in the worst-case scenario proved in Appendix B in
(
Raginsky et al. 2017
)
.

The first term of equation
64
can be bounded via the weighted CKP inequality

\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})\leq C_{\hat{p}_{K\eta}}\left[\sqrt{D_{KL}\left(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta}\right)}+\left(\frac{D_{KL}\left(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta}\right)}{2}\right)^{1/4}\right],

where the constant
C_{\hat{p}_{K\eta}}=2\inf\limits_{\lambda>0}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}\left(\frac{3}{2}+\text{log}\int\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{\lambda\left\|\omega\right\|^{2}}\hat{P}_{K\eta}(d\omega)\right)\right)
. By Lemma 4 in
Raginsky et al. 2017
and assuming
K\eta>1
, we can wrtie:

\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})\leq\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)K\eta\right)\left(D_{KL}\left(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta}\right)+\sqrt{D_{KL}\left(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta}\right)}\right).

Now we bound the term
D_{KL}\left(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta}\right)
. The Radon-Nikodym derivative of the
\hat{P}_{K\eta}
w.r.t
p_{K}
is the following

\frac{d\hat{p}_{K\eta}}{dp_{K}}=exp\left\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s})d\mathbf{B}s-\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}\|ds\right\}.

Thus, we have:

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{p_{K}}\left[\log\left(\frac{d\hat{p}_{K\eta}}{dp_{K}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{K\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M^{2}}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right].

(66)

We now bound the first term in the RHS of the equation (
66
), from the update rule in (
63
) we know:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}

\displaystyle=-(s-k\eta)\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\sqrt{2}\left(B_{s}-B_{k\eta}\right)

\displaystyle=-(s-k\eta)\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+(s-k\eta)\left(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right)+\sqrt{2}\left(B_{s}-B_{k\eta}\right),

thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]+3\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-\tilde{g}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]+6\eta d

\displaystyle\leq 3\eta^{2}\left(M\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|\right]+G\right)^{2}+3\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+6\eta d.

(67)

Similarly, we need a uniform bound of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
.

Lemma 15
.

Under assumptions
1
,
2
and
3
, if we set the step size
\eta\in\left(0,1\wedge\frac{m_{2}}{2M^{2}}\right)
, then for all
k\geq 0
, the
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\textnormal{v}}{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
can be bounded as

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\mathcal{E}+\frac{2\left(M^{2}+1\right)\Delta^{2}d}{4m_{2}},

provided
\mathcal{E}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{M}{m_{2}}\left(2b+2\eta G^{2}+2d\right).

The proof of Lemma
15
can be found in Appendix
F.4
. Using this bound, we can further bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\right\|^{2}\right]
as:

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\right\|^{2}\right]&\leq 6\eta^{2}M^{2}\left(\mathcal{E}+\frac{2\left(M^{2}+1\right)}{m_{2}}\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}\right)+6\eta^{2}G^{2}+3\eta^{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+6\eta d\\
&\leq 6\eta^{2}M^{2}\mathcal{E}+6\eta^{2}G^{2}+6\eta d+\left(\frac{12\eta^{2}M^{2}\left(M^{2}+1\right)}{m_{2}}+3(M^{2}+1)\right)\eta^{2}\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+3\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\\
&=:\overline{\mathcal{E}}\eta+C\eta^{2}\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+3\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}\end{aligned},

where the costant
\mathcal{E}
and
C
are defined as:

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle=6M^{2}\mathcal{E}+6G^{2}+6d

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\frac{12\eta^{2}M^{2}\left(M^{2}+1\right)}{m_{2}}+3(M^{2}+1).

Thus the divergence can be bounded as:

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M^{2}}{2}\left(\overline{\mathcal{E}}+C\eta\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+3\eta\sigma^{2}\right)K\eta^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left((M^{2}+1)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)K\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{M^{2}}{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}K\eta^{2}+\left(\frac{M^{2}}{2}C\eta^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(M^{2}+1)\right)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}K\eta+\frac{3M^{2}\eta^{2}+1}{2}\sigma^{2}K\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{M^{2}}{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}K\eta^{2}+\left(\frac{M^{2}}{2}C+\frac{1}{2}(M^{2}+1)\right)\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}K\eta+\frac{3M^{2}+1}{2}\sigma^{2}K\eta

\displaystyle=:C_{0}K\eta^{2}+C_{1}\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}K\eta+C_{2}\sigma^{2}K\eta.

We are ready to bound the Wasserstein distance,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left((C_{0}+\sqrt{C_{0}})\sqrt{\eta}+\left(C_{1}+\sqrt{C_{1}}\right)A+\left(C_{2}+\sqrt{C_{2}}\right)B\right)\left(K\eta\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=:\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}\sqrt{\eta}+\widetilde{C_{1}}^{2}A+\widetilde{C_{2}}^{2}B\right)\left(K\eta\right)^{2},

where the constants are defined as:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left\{\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4},\sqrt{\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}}\right\}

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\text{max}\left\{\sigma^{2},\sqrt{\sigma^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left(C_{0}+\sqrt{C_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left(C_{1}+\sqrt{C_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left(C_{2}+\sqrt{C_{2}}\right).

From Proposition 9 in the paper
Raginsky et al. 2017
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(\hat{p}_{K\eta},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2C_{LS}\left(\log\left\|p_{0}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{d}{2}\log\frac{3\pi}{m}+\left(\frac{M\kappa_{0}}{3}+B\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}+G_{0}+\frac{b}{2}\log 3\right)\right)}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}

Finally, we can have

\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})\leq\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}\eta^{1/4}+\widetilde{C_{1}}\sqrt{A}+\widetilde{C_{2}}\sqrt{B}\right)K\eta+\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}.

(68)

To bound the Wasserstein distance, we need to set

\widetilde{C_{0}}K\eta^{5/4}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(69)

Solving the (
69
), we can have

K\eta=C_{LS}\log\left(\frac{2\widetilde{C_{3}}}{\epsilon}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta=\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{16\widetilde{C_{0}}^{4}\left(K\eta\right)^{4}}.

Combining these two we can have

\eta=\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{16\widetilde{C_{0}}^{4}C_{LS}^{4}\log^{4}\left(\frac{2\widetilde{C_{3}}}{\epsilon}\right)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K=\frac{16\widetilde{C_{0}}^{4}C_{LS}^{5}\log^{5}\left(\frac{2\widetilde{C_{3}}}{\epsilon}\right)}{\epsilon^{4}}.

Plugging
K
and
\eta
into (
68
) completes the proof.

E.8
Proof of Theorem
8

In this section we generalize the convergence analysis of SGLDLP-L in
Zhang et al. 2022
to non-log-concave target distribution. Following the same proof outlines in
Raginsky et al. 2017
. Recall the SGLDLP-L update rule (
2
) is the following,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=Q_{W}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\sqrt{2\eta}\xi_{k+1})

\displaystyle=:{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\sqrt{2\eta}\xi_{k+1}+\alpha_{k},

where
\alpha_{k}
is defined as:

\alpha_{k}=Q_{W}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\sqrt{2\eta}\xi_{k+1})-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\sqrt{2\eta}\xi_{k+1}.

Thus, we can define a continuous interpolation of the SGLDLP-L as:

{\mathbf{x}}_{t}={\mathbf{x}}_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}G_{s}ds+\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}dB(s)+\int_{0}^{t}\alpha(s)ds,

where
G_{s}=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\bm{1}_{\mathrm{s\in\left(k\eta,(k+1)\eta\right)}}
and
\alpha(s)=\sum\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{k}/\eta\bm{1}_{\mathrm{s\in\left(k\eta,(k+1)\eta\right)}}
.
By taking the difference of the interpolation with the discrete estimation of Langevin process in equation (
62
), we can derive the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the
\hat{p}_{K\eta}
w.r.t
p_{K}
as:

\frac{d\hat{p}_{K\eta}}{dp_{K}}=exp\left\{\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}-\alpha(s))d\mathbf{B}s-\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}-\alpha(s)\|^{2}ds\right\}.

Thus, the divergence can be computed as:

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{K\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-G_{s}-\alpha(s)\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))-\alpha_{k}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{s})-\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M^{2}}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]ds+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\int_{k\eta}^{(k+1)\eta}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}/\eta\right\|^{2}\right]ds.

(70)

From the same analysis in (
25
), we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]+3\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]+6\eta d

\displaystyle\leq 3\eta^{2}\left(M\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+G\right)^{2}+3\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+6\eta d.

Again, we need to derive a uniform bound of
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\xi_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\eta\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]+2\eta d+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})+\eta\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+2\eta d

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]+\eta^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})-Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))\right\|^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+2\eta d.

By plugging in the inequality we derived before:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta\nabla U({\mathbf{x}}_{k})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\left(1-2\eta m_{2}+2\eta^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+2\eta b+2\eta^{2}G^{2}.

we can have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\left(1-2\eta m_{2}+2\eta^{2}M^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+2\eta b+2\eta^{2}G^{2}+\frac{\eta^{2}\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]+2\eta d.

(71)

Thus for any
\eta\in(0,1\wedge\frac{m_{2}}{2M^{2}})
and
1-2\eta m_{2}+2\eta^{2}M^{2}>0
, we can bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]
for any
k>0
as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{2\left(m_{2}-\eta M^{2}\right)}\left(2b+2G^{2}+\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}+2d\right)+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}{2\eta\left(m_{2}-\eta M^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{m_{2}}\left(2b+2G^{2}+\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}+2d\right)+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}{\eta m_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}+\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4m_{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}{\eta m_{2}},

where the constant
\mathcal{E}
is defined as:

\mathcal{E}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{m_{2}}\left(2b+2G^{2}+\sigma^{2}+2d\right).

Thus, we can have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{s}-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle 6\eta^{2}\left(\mathcal{E}+\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4m_{2}}+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}{\eta m_{2}}\right)+6\eta^{2}G^{2}+3\eta^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)+6\eta d

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{E}}\eta+3\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{6+3m_{2}}{4m_{2}}\eta^{2}\Delta^{2}d+\frac{6\eta\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}{m_{2}}.

Plugging this into the equation (
70
), we can have,

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}K\eta^{2}+\frac{3M\sigma^{2}K\eta^{3}}{4}+\frac{\left(6+3m_{2}\right)M\Delta^{2}d}{16m_{2}}K\eta^{3}+\frac{6M\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]K\eta^{2}}{4m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}+\sigma^{2}\right)K\eta+\frac{K\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]}{4\eta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}K\eta^{2}+\frac{3M+1}{4}\sigma^{2}K\eta+\frac{\left(\left(6+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}\Delta^{2}K\eta+\left(\frac{6M\eta}{4m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4\eta}\right)K\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right].

By the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{\Delta^{2}d}{4}
, we can further bound the divergence as:

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}K\eta^{2}+\frac{3M+1}{4}\sigma^{2}K\eta+\left(\frac{\left(\left(12+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}+\frac{d}{16\eta}\right)\Delta^{2}K

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle C_{0}K\eta^{2}+C_{1}\sigma^{2}K\eta+C_{2}\Delta^{2}K,

where the constants are defined as:

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{3M+1}{4}

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\left(\left(12+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}+\frac{d}{16\eta}\right).

We are ready to bound the Wasserstein distance,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left[\left(C_{0}+\sqrt{C_{0}}+\left(C_{1}+\sqrt{C_{1}}\right)A\right)\left(K\eta\right)^{2}+\left(C_{2}+\sqrt{C_{2}}\right)\Delta K^{2}\eta\right]

\displaystyle=:\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}\sqrt{\eta}+\widetilde{C_{1}}^{2}A\right)\left(K\eta\right)^{2}+\widetilde{C_{2}}^{2}\Delta K^{2}\eta,

where the constants are defined as:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\sigma^{2},\sqrt{\sigma^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left(C_{0}+\sqrt{C_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left(C_{1}+\sqrt{C_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{C_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(12+8\left(\kappa_{0}+2b+2d\right)\right)\left(C_{2}+\sqrt{C_{2}}\right).

From Proposition 9 in the paper
Raginsky et al. 2017
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(\hat{p}_{K\eta},p^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2C_{LS}\left(\log\left\|p_{0}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{d}{2}\log\frac{3\pi}{m}+\left(\frac{M\kappa_{0}}{3}+B\sqrt{\kappa_{0}}+G_{0}+\frac{b}{2}\log 3\right)\right)}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}

Finally, we can have

\mathcal{W}_{2}(p_{K},p^{*})\leq\left(\widetilde{C_{0}}\eta^{1/4}+\widetilde{C_{1}}\sqrt{A}\right)K\eta+\widetilde{C_{2}}\sqrt{\Delta}\sqrt{K^{2}\eta}+\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}.

(72)

To bound the
2
-Wasserstein distance, we need to set

\widetilde{C_{0}}K\eta^{5/4}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widetilde{C_{3}}e^{-K\eta/C_{LS}}=\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

(73)

Solving the (
73
), we can have

K\eta=C_{LS}\log\left(\frac{2\widetilde{C_{3}}}{\epsilon}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{16\widetilde{C_{0}}^{4}\left(K\eta\right)^{4}}.

Combining these two we can have

\eta\leq\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{16\widetilde{C_{0}}^{4}C_{LS}^{4}\log^{4}\left(\frac{2\widetilde{C_{3}}}{\epsilon}\right)}\quad\mbox{and}\quad K\geq\frac{16\widetilde{C_{0}}^{4}C_{LS}^{5}\log^{5}\left(\frac{2\widetilde{C_{3}}}{\epsilon}\right)}{\epsilon^{4}}.

Plugging
K
and
\eta
into (
72
) completes the proof.

E.9
Proof of Theorem
9

In this section we generalize the convergence analysis of VC SGLDLP-F in
Zhang et al. 2022
to non-log-concave target distribution. The proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
8
, but the variance corrected-quantization function
The variance-corrected quantization technique establishes a scalable bound for the discrepancy between quantized and full-precision values, contingent on the learning rate.
This enables variance-corrected quantization advantage over simple stochastic rounding.

Recall that the update of VC SGLDLP-L is

\displaystyle{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1}

\displaystyle=Q^{vc}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})),2\eta,\Delta\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\sqrt{2\eta}\xi_{k}+\alpha_{k},

where
\alpha_{k}
is defined as

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}

\displaystyle=Q^{vc}\left({\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k})),2\eta,\Delta\right)-{\mathbf{x}}_{k}-\eta Q_{G}(\widetilde{\nabla U}({\mathbf{x}}_{k}))+\sqrt{2\eta}\xi_{k}.

From analysis in
Zhang et al. 2022
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\max\left(2\Delta\eta G,5\eta d\right)

\displaystyle=:\eta A.

Combining the analysis in section
E.8
, we can show,

\displaystyle D_{KL}(p_{K}||\hat{p}_{K\eta})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}K\eta^{2}+\frac{3M+1}{4}\sigma^{2}K\eta+\frac{\left(\left(6+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}\Delta^{2}K\eta+\left(\frac{6M\eta}{4m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4\eta}\right)K\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\alpha_{k}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}K\eta^{2}+\frac{3M+1}{4}\sigma^{2}K\eta+\frac{\left(\left(6+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}\Delta^{2}K\eta+\left(\frac{6M\eta}{4m_{2}}+\frac{1}{4\eta}\right)K\eta A

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}K\eta^{2}+\frac{3M+1}{4}\sigma^{2}K\eta+\frac{\left(\left(6+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}\Delta^{2}K\eta+\frac{6M+m_{2}}{m_{2}}KA

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle C_{0}K\eta^{2}+C_{1}K\eta\sigma^{2}+C_{2}K\eta\Delta^{2}+C_{3}KA,

where the constant
C_{0}
,
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
C_{3}
are defined as:

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{M\overline{\mathcal{E}}}{4}

\displaystyle C_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{3M+1}{4}

\displaystyle C_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\left(6+3m_{2}\right)M+m_{2}\right)d}{16m_{2}}

\displaystyle C_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{6M+m_{2}}{m_{2}}

We are ready to bound the Wasserstein distance,

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(p_{K},\hat{p}_{K\eta})
